- Region analysis (0.4.0 audit 05, found during A #4 design, 2026-11-25): `.index` returns `t25WithoutOwners(target)` (`src/semantics/region.byn`, computeBorrow `.index`), so an element copy loses its owners: `ys.append(xs.span); let t = ys[0]; grow xs; t[0]` and the `arr[0] = xs.span` variant check clean and are use-after-free (probes /Users/sy/Developer/ikhbat.org/bayan/tmp/a/p/f6, f10). Fix: an element of a list/array/span whose element type may borrow keeps the owners other than the collection itself; expect new reports in src.
- LOW (A #1 review, fix/region-soundness 0f9b45087): the first-store hook in `t41StoreBorrow` (`src/semantics/region2.byn`, the `else` branch's `t25Changed`) has no covering case; three probes (tmp/a-p/p1–p3 in the A worktree) did not reach it, and deleting it fails no case.
- LOW (A #1 review): `r.environments` is read when filtering call results (`src/semantics/region2.byn` ~:210) without `t25Read`; it only removes regions, so no soundness loss is known, but the dirty rule does not cover it.
- LOW (A #1 review 3): `tests/cases/diag/region-fixpoint-for-variable.byn` proves convergence only on a program the binder rejects (BYN2104, loop variables are immutable); on valid code `referBorrow` never reaches a loop variable, so the union in `t25For` has no valid-code case.
- Effects (review of fix/effects-direct-flag, LOW x3): no case pins the BYN6004 span for a function without a declaration (a lambda: `u.span`, src/semantics/effects.byn:283) now that a direct use replaces an indirect one; no case pins the BYN6002 order when a replacement moves a use later (fs at line 4, alloc at 5, `Io.readText` at 6 now reports alloc first); BYN6002's help "or remove the call" (effects.byn:247) is wrong when the direct `alloc` is a list/map literal or interpolation.
- Windows bayan part (wlt1, --jobs 16, 2026-11-25, D 8781b026): `help-language: golden helps start with a verb`, L002 and L023 time out at 60 s under load; alone on mb1 they take 7–39 s on dev and D alike. Raise their per-test bound under BAYAN_TEST_TIME_SCALE or run them in a serial tail.
- Region #4(c) review-12 (MEDIUM, pre-existing): a read through another name for a span view is not seen after a store. `sp[0] = xs.span; xs.append…; print(arr[0][0])` (the backing array) and `let sp2 = sp` before the store, then `sp2[0][0]`, both check clean and read freed memory; the store's loan holder is only `sp`. Needs alias tracking between a span view and its backing local (probes tmp/rv12/st-alias, st-alias2 in the fix-region-soundness worktree).
- Region #4(c) review-12 (LOW): loans are never killed, so overwriting the slot (`sp[0] = xs.span; sp[0] = ys.span`, then mutating `xs`) or reassigning `sp` (`sp = arr2.mutableSpan()`) after the store gives a false BYN3004. Same flow-insensitive class as #4(a); fold into its whole-store dominance.
- Region #4(c) review-12 (LOW, pre-existing): a store into a parameter span inside a callee (`put(sp, by: xs.span)`) gives the caller no loan; the store summary records no span-element target.
- Region #4(c) review-12 (LOW): the `created` comment in `t41StoreIntoBorrow` (region2.byn) says "the later of the lend point and the store" but the code takes the lend point only when it falls inside the store's span, else the store's end; reword the comment.
- Region #5 review-12 (LOW, pre-existing): `.mapCopy` is not in the no-argument fresh-allocation case of `builtinBorrow` (region.byn), so `elements` is unused there; unreachable today (`Map.copy()` without `to:` is BYN2301).
- Region #5 (LOW): a span copy finds the list it views by type (a lent list or array local with the receiver's element type) because `Borrow` keeps no lend chain; an element source that is itself such a list would be dropped. Record the viewed storage on the lend instead.
- Regions (review 13 of fix/region-soundness, LOW): `let s = this.items.span; return s.copy()` (or a span local over a temporary list) is still refused with BYN3003 "the temporary on line N"; only a direct view (`this.items.span.copy()`) takes the list's borrow (`viewBases` in src/semantics/region.byn). The span-local form falls to the element-type test, which sees `this` (a struct), not the list.
- Regions (review 14 of fix/region-soundness, MEDIUM, pre-existing): `changedFields` (src/semantics/region2.byn) misses a callee that reassigns a field (`this.xs = List<i64>()`), so `obj.reset(obj.xs.span)` checks clean while the callee frees the view's storage; BYN3005 for a `ref this` receiver relies on it (the local-receiver filter and the `this` path both).
- Regions (review 14 of fix/region-soundness, LOW): BYN3004 for a field of `this` names the field bare (`a`) where BYN3005 now says `this.a` (`t41RootName`); use the same name in both.
- Regions (review 15 of fix/region-soundness, LOW, pre-existing): BYN3005 for a `ref this` call on a field path receiver (`arr[0].f(arr[0].ys.span)`, `obj.a.f(obj.a.ys.span)`) still reports when the callee leaves the viewed field alone; the field filter in `callBorrow` (src/semantics/region2.byn) runs only for a bare local receiver. Needs the argument's field path compared relative to the receiver. The C# is clean (probe tmp/rv15/p12 in the fix-region-soundness worktree).
- Regions (review 15, LOW): `strBufAppend` and `strBufGrow` are missing from the mutating builtins in `directChanges` (src/semantics/region2.byn), so `changedFields` misses a callee that grows a StrBuf field.
- Regions (#4(a) gate, LOW): a back-edge store in a bare block (`{ s = xs.span }`) counts the block as a scope, so a later read the store always dominates is BYN3004 (tests/cases/diag/region-loop-block-store pins it); a bare block always runs and could pass its parent's scope through (`checkMutations` in src/semantics/region2.byn).
- Parser (LOW): a parameter named `uses` gives BYN1001 "expected `)`" at the `<` of its type instead of naming the clash with the `uses` keyword.
- Regions (review 16 of fix/region-soundness, LOW, pre-existing): a `ref this` call through a writable `dyn ref` local (`let d: dyn ref IGrow = obj; d.f(obj.xs.span)`) is never checked for BYN3005, and runs a stale read (probe tmp/rv16/b1 in the fix-region-soundness worktree); the receiver field filter would also skip it, since an interface method's changed fields never name `obj`'s fields.
- Regions (review 16 of fix/region-soundness, LOW): `backEdgeUse` (src/semantics/region2.byn) is about cubic in store/use pairs of one holder in one loop (n=800 generated pairs: 2.88 s vs 0.17 s before the rule); the driver self-check is unchanged. Fable: once `closes` depends only on (mutation, holder, loop), a one-entry cache per mutation removes a factor of n; re-measure after round 2.
- Region analysis (review-17 LOW 3, 2026-11-25): the forward rule (loan made before the mutation) treats an enclosing loop as running to its end, so `for k { for i { xs.append(i); s = xs.span }; print(s.length); for j { if s.length > 0 { print(s[0]) }; xs.append(j); s = xs.span } }` reports "used again" at the outer loop's closing brace (probe .worktrees/fix-region-soundness/tmp/rv17/f07; same before round 2). False positive, not a miss.
- Region analysis (Fable ruling on review-17, 2026-11-25): an early exit with a defer inside the loop is not seen by the back-edge rule. `for { defer use(s); xs.append(i); return if c; s = xs.span }` checks clean, and round 3's `own` scope extends the gap to nested scopes (`if c { defer use(s); M; return if d; s = xs.span }`, reported before round 3, clean after). `return`/`fail` are not counted jumps; counting them breaks reads after the loop. A structural definite-store walk (Fable: open it if review 18 finds another class) would replace the positional rounds.
- Region analysis (review-18 LOW, 2026-11-25): the back-edge rule tries only M's own scope and P's body as the closing scope, so a whole store in a scope between them is never chosen: `for k { for i { if i > 2 { for j { xs.append(j) }; s = xs.span } }; read s }` and `if { if { M }; s = xs.span }; read s` are reported though every path stores (probes .worktrees/fix-region-soundness/tmp/rv18/c07, b03, a06). False positive.
- Region analysis (Fable round-2/3 rule, review-18 HIGH fixed in b4c84107e, 2026-11-25): review 18 found a fourth class of positional miss in the loop back-edge rule (jumps before the mutation that run after it). Fable's condition is met: open a structural definite-store walk (per-path "holder stored since mutation" dataflow over the loop body) to replace checkMutations' positional closes/lower/upper tests; it would also cover the two LOW false positives above and the defer early-exit gap.
